# Multi-Level Work Breakdown Structure (WBS)

> **Companion design document**. This document is a companion to
> [RFC-039 Compiler Architecture Refactoring](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md). It
> expands the RFC's phase sequence into **three levels of tasks that can be committed and verified
> independently**.
>
> RFC-039's main text is the sole authority for phase order and acceptance gates. This document is
> responsible only for decomposition to an executable granularity and for registering conflicts that
> have not yet been adjudicated.

## Positioning and Scope

| Level       | Count                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ----------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Level 1** | 11 (P0–P10)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Phases, in one-to-one correspondence with RFC-039's phase sequence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| **Level 2** | 72 (2026-10-13: 53 +2 = 3.13 / 3.14; 2026-10-14: +1 = 3.15; 2026-10-14: +1 = 3.16; 2026-10-15: +2 = 3.17 / 3.18; 2026-10-16: +1 = 0.4; 2026-10-18: +3 = 3.19 / S7 / S8; 2026-10-18: +2 = 3.21 / S9 (this group was originally assigned 3.20; on 2026-10-21 it was moved to 3.21, and 3.20 was reassigned to “Existing false positives in dead-code analysis”; see the closure record in [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)); 2026-10-21: +2 = 0.5 / 3.20; 2026-10-21: +1 = S10 (the S9 number proposed for the 4.4 batch was already used by “Abstract closure of pre-release audit findings” and was therefore deferred); 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.22; 2026-10-25: +1 = 3.23; 2026-10-26: +1 = 9.12; 2026-10-27: +1 = S11)                                                                                                                                  | Task groups derived from the “Implementation Points” sections of the various documents + the 2026-10-07 audit supplement (P3's 3.4) + the 2026-10-10/11 adjudication supplements (P3's 3.5/3.6/3.7/3.8/3.9) + the 2026-10-12 registration (P3's 3.10) + the 2026-10-12 discovery supplement (P3's 3.11) + the 2026-10-12 additional discovery supplement (P3's 3.12) |
| **Level 3** | 194 (2026-10-13: 144 +2 = 3.13.1 / 3.14.1; 2026-10-14: +2 = 3.15.1 / 3.16.1; 2026-10-15: +2 = 3.17.1 / 3.18.1; 2026-10-16: +2 = 4.6.2 / 4.9.6 (`#450`, user adjudication A); 2026-10-16: +1 = 0.4.1; 2026-10-17: +1 = 4.9.7 (`#464`); 2026-10-18: +3 = 3.19.1 / S7.1 / S8.1; 2026-10-18: +12 = 4.9.8 / 3.14.2 / 4.10.4 / 4.10.5 / 3.21.1–3.21.4 (formerly 3.20.1–3.20.4, renumbered on 2026-10-21) / S9.1 / S9.2 / S2.2 / 4.4.4; 2026-10-21: +7 = 3.6.2 / 3.6.3 / 3.6.4 / 0.5.1 / 0.5.2 / 3.20.1 / 3.20.2; 2026-10-21: +1 = S10.1; 2026-10-22: +5 = 3.6.5 / 3.6.6 / 4.10.6 / 4.10.7 / 4.10.8; 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.11.3; 2026-10-24: +1 = 0.5.3; 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.22.1; 2026-10-25: +2 = 3.23.1 / 3.18.2; +2 = 3.20.3 / 3.18.3; 2026-10-26: +1 = 3.20.4; 2026-10-26: +1 = 9.12.1; +1 = 9.12.2; 2026-10-27: +1 = S11.1; 2026-10-31: +1 = 3.20.5) | Independently committable actions                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

**Reserved numbers (not reclaimed)**: P4 has no `4.7`/`4.8`, and P8 has no `8.1`. None of the three
has had a corresponding entry since the WBS was established—the full history of this document has
zero matches for the three headings `**4.7` / `**4.8` / `**8.1` (this can be verified with
`git log -S'**4.7' -- docs/src/dev/architecture/09-execution-wbs.md`), and there are no references
to them anywhere else in the repository. `8.1` corresponds precisely to phase 1 of `05` (test
revival and completion). That phase has been split into P1 (`1.1`–`1.3`) and `8.8`; therefore, P8's
numbering is aligned with its phase numbers (phase 0 → `8.0`, phase 2 → `8.2`, and so forth).

**Covered by this document**: Task decomposition, dependency relationships, parallelization groups,
and binding of acceptance criteria.

**Not covered by this document**: The design rationale for tasks (see `02`–`08`) and the rationale
for phase order (see RFC-039).

**Registration convention (effective 2026-10-12)**: Every newly registered Level 2/Level 3 row
**must contain two fields**: “**Owning phase**” (the P to which it truly belongs, which may differ
from the containing section) and “**Blocks the mainline**”. `Pending scheduling` is no longer the
default state; an unmarked entry is deemed **not schedulable**. Numbers must be **unique throughout
the document** (cross-track tasks use their track number, such as `029g.x`, and must not occupy an
`N.x` number). **Checkoff/completion records are based on “task ID + evidence (gate values, run ID,
issue number, file:line)” and do not include commit numbers**—`pull --rebase` rewrites local hashes,
so a hard-coded number inevitably becomes invalid (2026-10-13 example: `94ad03ce4`→`631eb3e3c`).
Look up commit numbers with `git log`.

## Current State: Gaps Found During Decomposition

While consolidating the “Implementation Points” sections of eight documents into this table, **five
hard gaps in the documentation system itself** were found. All have now been closed:

| #   | Gap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Resolution                                                                                                                                                            |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| G1  | **P5 has no implementation steps whatsoever.** RFC defines P5 (checker decomposition) and its acceptance criterion (C1 zero-diff), but none of the seven documents describes how to perform the decomposition; the directory tree in `01-routing.md` contains one `annotations.rs` entry, the only mention in the entire batch.                | P5 steps are supplied by this document (see below)                                                                                                                    |
| G2  | **The three ownership assignments for the `include!` refactor have no implementation site.** RFC lists it under P4; `02` assigns it to itself; S7 in `06` also lists it. Yet the change list in `02` (6 additions + 20 modifications) **does not contain `semantic_tokens.rs`**.                                                               | Implementation steps are assigned to §P5 5.1 in this document (after P4 completes); references in RFC and 06 have been synchronized to point here                     |
| G3  | **Three coexisting lists of CI scripts.** `RFC` lists four `scripts/ci/*.py`; `01` lists four scripts + one `cargo test`; `07` lists four different checks; `04` also requires `check-synth-boundary.py`. After deduplication, there are **at least nine new scripts and no unified list**; `check-obligations.py` is owned by both P4 and P9. | The unified list appears in §P9 of this document (11 scripts, each owned by one phase); all other documents refer to that table                                       |
| G4  | **Conflict over the fate of `precedence_inline.rs`.** `06` counts it among the “1005 lines that need to be revived,” while B6 in the same document says it “must be deleted together with the `Precedence` enum”; `05` advocates deletion.                                                                                                     | **Revive in P1, delete with Pratt in P8** (decision registered as C1)—first restore CI coverage, then perform the deletion in 8.6 as part of P8's dead-ladder cleanup |
| G5  | **Conflicting wording for the L2 objective.** RFC and this directory's index say “grammar-driven syntax,” but `05` self-assesses as “**not using true grammar-driven parsing (LALRPOP); only declarative table lookup was implemented**”.                                                                                                      | Converged on **full LALRPOP grammar-driven parsing** (`05` §Syntax: full grammar-driven parsing); G7/G8 acceptance gates are enforced against that commitment         |

## Target Design: Phase Sequence

```
P0  仓库维护机制与代码放置规程     08        ← 新增，先立规矩
P1  复活孤儿测试并修缺陷           06 §S1
P2  建立等价性判据基线             07 全部
P3  修正确性漏洞（最小方案）        02 §S3-漏洞半部
P4  阶段契约与统一 Driver          02
P5  checker 文件内拆分             02 + 本文补齐
P6  类型表示单一化                 03
P7  中间表示 SSA 化                04      } 可并行
P8  前端范式变更                   05      }
P9  防反弹门禁（含统一脚本清单）     01 §防反弹 + 08 §可机检规则
P10 其余清理与状态修正             06 §S2/S3/S4/S5/S6
```

**Rationale for adding P0**: The three prohibitions—no fabrication, no endless filling, and no
patching when a refactor is required—constrain every action in P1–P10. The rules must be established
before work starts; otherwise, P1 would already be adding `mod` declarations to `tests/mod.rs` while
reviving tests, which is precisely the cause of the G4 conflict.

The fix = reduce `stderr` to `diagnostics: [[code, file, line], …]` + retain only **crash marker lines** (panic / ICE / backtrace header); add `strip_ansi` (run side carries color codes) and lookahead for positions (positions remain obtainable when source-code snippets appear between the diagnostic header and `-->`); `exit` / `stdout` / `check.*` / `expectation` remain unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | 0.5.1         | The 356-line baseline is regenerated, `check-corpus-parity` reports no differences; **rerunning the probe after changing locale text still produces zero differences** (rationale verified by measurement); mutation verification: changing one `run.exit` to 99 reports one difference; cost = the corpus layer no longer verifies runtime text; this is instead handled by locale unit tests / `yx explain` snapshots / generated-artifact reconciliation; **Owning phase: P0; Blocks the mainline: No**                                         |
|                                               | 0.5.2 Channel integration and calibration: hook `cargo test` into pre-commit and have CI use the `fast` (before local commit) and `full` (before push and CI baseline) dual channels; calibrate the 2600-tier resource profile from measurements (currently extrapolated); add a section on resource markers and exclusive tiers to `test-specification.md` (**pending scheduling**; follow-up slice of #486)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | 0.5.1         | The `cargo test` hook in `pre-commit` no longer runs the full suite bare (currently `cargo test --all` runs everything; the “Common Commands” section of AGENTS.md now specifies the fast/full dual channels and the red line against running the full `--ignored` suite bare); CI and the pre-commit local path use the same channel baseline; the 2600-tier marker's peak memory and duration are measured rather than extrapolated; `test-specification.md` has a corresponding detailed section; **Owning phase: P0; Blocks the mainline: No** |

> **There is no scale-baseline task.** The 2026-10-03 decision cancelled all line-count / size
> gates; scale issues are handled through separation of responsibilities (see `08` Prohibition 2
> Criterion A, which requires human judgment). **0.2.1 is the core acceptance criterion for this
> phase**: if the gate cannot report the known parallel representations, the criterion design is
> wrong and the gate is false.

### P3 Fix Correctness Vulnerabilities (Minimal Solution)

Source: [02](02-stage-contract.md) §Implementation Points S3, vulnerability-fix half + **2026-10-07
audit of silent-drop points (3.4)**

> **Semantic-correction routing (D59, user adjudication on 2026-10-11)**: If this track or a later
> phase discovers a defect for which “the old behavior did not count as success”—**A silent failure
> / B silent mismatch / C undefined behavior and resource loss** (crash, stack exhaustion, or
> silently returning the wrong answer)—it **may be fixed alongside the refactor**, but all four
> requirements must be met: ① red first, then green (the red criterion must exist first and actually
> be red); ② do not change the externally observable behavior of programs that “previously compiled
> successfully and ran correctly” (they must not start failing, and their existing diagnostic codes
> / text / exit codes must not be removed or changed); ③ register it in the same commit (this row
> identifies the error type + adds a revision note for that phase + includes the “Error Type” line
> in the PR's required block); ④ regenerate the baseline in the same commit + manually review the
> diff. **D user-visible semantics / E external contract tables / F RFC-level design changes must
> still receive user adjudication first.** See
> [07 §Routing Criteria for Semantic Corrections](07-equivalence-oracle.md) for the expanded
> criteria and A–F whitelist; **“default super-refactor + post-hoc registration” is not adopted**
> (rationale: RFC-039 D59—a zero-red criterion cannot distinguish a fix from a regression and
> conflicts with “no C5′” and “no relaxation whatsoever”).

      registration—the typecheck resolution loop could already select the right candidate by name
      (proven by trace), and the false rejection came from an unknown-name **precheck** in the arity
      settlement block (reading the first candidate's name table from the metadata array and
      ignoring slots that had already been bound successfully); after fixing typecheck, a second
      layer surfaced in ir_gen (`lookup_param_info` looks up by leaf name in `fn_param_names`, and
      the last definition wins for duplicate names, so a resolution hit on `Temp.pick` nevertheless
      binds parameter names from the last candidate `Temp.pick#1`). Fix surface: move the precheck
      ahead of slot binding and populate `available` with the union of all candidate parameter
      names; the fallback path filters arity-compatible candidates by “name compatibility” (sharing
      `names_support_named_args` with the resolution loop, single criterion source), and the
      signature of the unique compatible candidate follows through (E1002 is attributed to the
      candidate that owns the name); ir_gen registers mixed names first / looks up mixed names
      first. Red first, then green: freeze parent commit `10a2dbcb`, cover the new test with 4 reds
      → `4941f443`, 23 green (acceptance matrix 9/9b/10/11, `named_args.rs`); zero corpus
      differences (the D59 category C prerequisite holds); gates: fast two waves exit 0, clippy 0
      errors, four CI-only gates pass, error-code generated artifacts regenerate for line shifts in
      the same commit; all 8 review items PASS (measured on the frozen revision by the mainline on
      behalf of the reviewer—the review subagents died twice due to infrastructure quota/concurrency
      limits, and the orchestration deviation was recorded faithfully)

      [#509](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/509)); **the entire group was archived on
      2026-10-25**: all four Level 3 tasks passed verification/review and were pushed, all local
      gates were green, and CI conclusions from each batch were reconciled according to coverage
      overlap (those still running were folded into the pre-release unified check; see the closure
      comments on the relevant issues); **Owning phase: P3; Blocks the mainline: No**)

- [x] **3.22 Decomposition semantics for function-type annotations** (1 Level 3 task, registered on
      2026-10-24, issue [#501](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/501); source = F4
      investigation during the 4.10 round-2 batch review; **3.22.1 completed on 2026-10-30**
      (implementation `7880ee17` + fix round `f032582b`—see the table below and
      [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md) for the criterion chain, three fix rounds, and two new
      registrations); **Owning phase: P3; Blocks the mainline: No**)
- [x] **3.23 Silent degradation on manifest parse failure / missing required fields** (1 Level 3
      task, registered on 2026-10-25, issue
      [#503](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/503); source = measurement during 4.9.8
      implementation—when `yaoxiang.toml` fails to parse (invalid TOML) or lacks a required field
      (such as `[package] version`), the CLI **does not report a manifest error** and silently
      degrades to single-file semantics ⇒ the cross-file reference pool becomes ineffective ⇒ false
      `W1002`×2 (`a.yx:1:1` / `b.yx:1:1`; both types are referenced by the entry point), while
      `check` still exits 0; pre-fix and post-4.9.8-fix behavior matched item by item (predates #465
      and is unrelated to this fix); D59 **category D** (externally observable behavior), semantics
      pending adjudication (A report an error and exit / B continue but emit a W code / C preserve
      current behavior); **Owning phase: P3; Blocks the mainline: No**)

> **Batch (user adjudication on 2026-10-14)**: The four small items 3.13 / 3.14 / 3.15 / 3.16 are
> **highly related** (method binding, resolution, and the inference entry point belong to one path;
> 3.16 is another carrier of the same “named argument” criterion—stdlib native exports), and **are
> merged into one team**—the scope is broadened from “one Level 2 task” to “a batch of related small
> items”; the batch composition, rationale, and deviations must be registered when that team closes
> (transcript in [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)).

Terminal criterion (semantics pending adjudication: A report an error and exit / B continue but emit a W code / C preserve current behavior, to be finalized after user adjudication): an unreadable manifest **must not silently degrade**—the manifest problem must be explicitly reported (error and exit, or a W code), and the `W1002` false positives caused by the degradation must disappear; red first, then green: one red criterion for each of the missing `version` / invalid TOML forms (red state = zero manifest diagnostics + two `W1002` false positives); (**closure registered on 2026-10-30**: implementation `81d86810` (19 files +415/−77)—role_context becomes three-state `RoleContext { surfaces, test_rules, manifest_diagnostics }`: no manifest (NotFound) = valid single-file mode unchanged; readable manifest = existing assembly; **present but unreadable** (other I/O errors / invalid TOML / missing required fields) ⇒ new code **E3024** (error level, with the manifest path and TOML details in the message, span_exempt—#324 contract). **Decision A** (2026-10-30, with full authority delegated and recorded): treat this the same as invalid .yx source—report an error and abort; the diagnostic is injected through the RoleClassification arm (Check/Lsp gated, matching the E3020 precedent; the MultiFile run channel only installs W codes, so no E3024 escapes, with the boundary registered) and the phase is marked failed, so the only phase depending on it, DeadCodeAnalysis, is skipped ⇒ degradation-related W1002 false positives disappear, and abort occurs before degradation; Typecheck and other independent arms still run. **Acceptance**: Vulnerability criteria turn green, and **deliberately removing the fix must make
them red again**. **Risk**: The new E4018 on the multi-file path is a breaking change.

**3.4 acceptance**: 3.4.1 / 3.4.2 must have red criteria first (changing code first is prohibited);
3.4.3 requires manual review; 3.4.4–3.4.8 use C2/C3 and P9's 9.2 gate. **Risk**: 3.4.1 adds a
diagnostic and is therefore a behavioral change; the corpus differential baseline and CHANGELOG must
be updated in sync.

> **Stop-the-bleeding path (decision on 2026-10-05)**: P3 does not have to wait for all of P2. The
> only hard prerequisites for 3.1/3.2 are **2.3.2 (multi-file corpus layer) + 2.4.1/2.4.3 (two red
> criteria)**; 2.1/2.2/2.3.1/2.3.3 (IR verifier, snapshots, single-file differential, performance
> baseline) can proceed in parallel with P3. Stopping the bleeding from correctness defects (such as
> `Sorted(3)` silently passing) must not be blocked by snapshot-infrastructure construction. 3.3
> (converting panic to diagnostic) has no prerequisites and can land at any time.

### External Track RFC-029g: Remove `pub` and Automatic Binding (External Track / #399 — Ordering Constraint D56)

Source: [RFC-029g](../../rfc/accepted/029g-remove-pub-and-auto-bind.md) (accepted on 2026-10-02;
implementation not started), [#399](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/399). **This track
is not a design item of the refactor itself; it is a specification-compliance deletion forced by an
ordering constraint**; it is distinct from P10 S5 “reduce the visibility of `pub` items” (Rust-style
`pub(crate)` narrowing).

**Why it must be completed in full before P4**: RFC-029g's deletion surface overlaps with the
rewrite surfaces of four refactor phases. Reversing the order means redoing the same deletions and
baseline updates on the new grammar / new checker layout / new SSA IR.

| 029g deletion surface                                              | Files                                                                                                              | Rewritten by which phase                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `KwPub` and syntax branches                                        | `lexer/{tokens,state,mod}.rs`; `parser/parser_state.rs`, `statements/{declarations,imports,functions,bindings}.rs` | **P8** (8.2 lexical convergence / 8.3 grammar construction / 8.6 Pratt deletion / 8.7 `parse_assign_after_target` split)                                                                                                                                                                 |
| AST `is_pub` ×2 (`Assign` / `TypeDefinition`)                      | `parser/ast.rs:247/270`                                                                                            | **P6 6.5 AST dead variants** + P8 + D1 directory rename (`parser/ast.rs` → top-level `src/ast/`)                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `auto_bind_to_type` and the pub branch of `collect_exports`        | `typecheck/checker.rs:2150/3802/3836`, `environment.rs:284`                                                        | **P5** (`include!`→true `mod` and checker decomposition are **pure moves**, and the pub branch is moved verbatim into the new file)                                                                                                                                                      |
| Dead-code exemption `exempt_pub` / `is_exported` and role dispatch | `typecheck/passes/dead_code.rs`, `module/orchestrator.rs:389-407` (inside `check_project`)                         | **P4 4.2.2** (`check_project` slimming)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `semantic_tokens`'s `Public` modifier                              | `typecheck/checker/semantic_tokens.rs:450/498`                                                                     | **P4 4.2.6** + P5 + 4.6 (D55)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

**Acceptance (behavioral changes; red criteria first)**:

1. **Red first, then green**: each of the two behavioral changes must have its red criterion land
   before code changes—① `pub` changes from “silently accepted” to a parse error; ② an unreferenced
   top-level binding in the Lib/Script role newly reports W1001 (previously pub items were
   absolutely exempt).
2. **The test count must not decrease**: when deleting the `exempt_pub` cases in
   `typecheck/passes/tests/dead_code.rs` and the `is_pub` fixtures, they must be replaced by
   equivalent new cases.
3. **Baseline in the same commit**: diagnostic-set changes (new parse errors + new W1001) ⇒ corpus
   differential baseline (`tests/baselines/corpus-parity.jsonl`) and CHANGELOG updated in the same
   commit; IR snapshots are expected to be zero-diff, and any non-zero difference requires an
   explanation.
4. **Independent revert unit**: the entire batch occupies one commit group and must not be mixed
   with other phases (DoD 6).
5. `cargo test` / `clippy -D warnings` / `python scripts/rfc/check_tracking.py` all green.

**Ordering**: It overlaps with 3.4 files (`checker.rs`, `environment.rs`) ⇒ the two must be serial
and cannot run in parallel; **the whole track precedes P4** (RFC-039 D56). The RFC-029g track does
not depend on 3.4; the implementer chooses which to do first based on the work at hand.

> **Commit cross-reference (2026-10-08)**: This section's incorporation (the P3.5 chapter +
> main-chain/constraint row) and RFC-039's D56 were swept into `97071c81b` together during
> parallel-stream workspace cleanup. That commit's message records “3.4 supplemental registration +
> D57/D58 adjudication filing” and does not mention this track. History is not rewritten; following
> the existing convention (`d5b18390` precedent, see the P2 landing note), it is registered in place
> as evidence for later bisecting.

of `collect_used_in_type` must not change)

**Hard constraint**: `collect_used_in_type` is used across modules by `inference/statements.rs:16`.
**If** the function is migrated with the annotation family in the future, `checker.rs` **must** add
a `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export so that the import remains unchanged; 5.3 **did not
migrate it** by measurement, so this batch added no re-export (the criterion was already satisfied).

**Only decompose confirmed large blocks.** Do not split modules that are only 200–400 lines—this
adds navigation cost, reduces cohesion, and yields zero decoupling benefit. The remaining submodules
(signatures / type_defs / imports) wait until their files grow back to 1,500 lines.

**Hard acceptance** (**2026-10-27 convention revised, inheriting user adjudication ② on
2026-10-26**): `git diff --stat tests/yaoxiang tests/baselines src/std/` must be **empty in every
round from 6.1 through 6.7**. **Direct-driver test files** under `tests/` (tests that directly drive
modified code, such as `tests/formatter_integration.rs`) **may be modified when mechanically
required**, but each modification must be registered with **file / line count / rationale**; **tests
must not be deleted and assertions must not be weakened**. **Exception**: 6.8.2 is a
**behavioral-fix** batch (new diagnostic code + baseline update), does not fall under this hard
acceptance criterion, and runs as a separately adjudicated round.

> **Revision relationship**: The original convention (user adjudication ② on 2026-10-26) was written
> as `git diff --stat tests/ src/std/`, making all of `tests/` subject to a zero-difference
> requirement. The revised convention **changes only direct-driver test files from “zero difference”
> to “may be modified but must register file / line count / rationale”**; the zero-difference
> requirements for the **corpus layer (`tests/yaoxiang`), baseline layer (`tests/baselines`), and
> standard library (`src/std/`)** remain unchanged. First application = 6.1.2: only
> `tests/formatter_integration.rs` changed, by **3 lines** (two `assert_format_eq` instances
> changing `i64`→`Int64` + one comment), with the rationale that the expected type name must be
> synchronized with the changed spelling; no test was deleted and no assertion was weakened. See 09a
> “P6 6.1 Name Canonicalization Closure”.

**Risk**: 8.4's dual-parser differential is an **unskippable equivalence gate**. If it finds a
non-equivalence, that must be resolved before the 8.5 switch, rather than hidden after the switch by
C5′. If any of 8.6's approximately 89 BP references are missed, they will **silently change
associativity** (no error, only a different parse result).

> **8.3–8.6 depend on D0/D2 from `08`**: a grammar file placed outside `parser/`, or action code
> that refers back to `sema`, is blocked by `check-boundary.py`.

### P9 Regression-Prevention Gates (Unified Script List — This Document Supplements Original Gap G3)

Source: [01](01-routing.md) §Regression-Prevention Mechanism + [08](08-maintenance-mechanism.md)
§Machine-Checkable Rules

**This is the sole authoritative list of all CI scripts** (11 scripts). Scripts are connected to CI
at their “introduction phase”; those marked report-only are converted to hard failures uniformly in
P9 (from “report” to “fail”). Existing violations (parallel representations, disambiguation aliases,
etc.) cannot be cleared until P6/P8; converting them to hard failures early would leave CI
permanently red and cause gates to be manually bypassed, so when P9 converts them, the gates should
be exactly green. P2's 2.5, P4's 4.3.2, and P7's 7c.1 refer to this table and do not define separate
lists.

- [ ] **9.1–9.11 Connect the eleven scripts at their introduction phases, then convert them
      uniformly to hard failures in P9** (details in the table below)
- [x] **9.12 Prerequisites for conversion to hard failure: rebaseline criterion-C wiring points and
      clear the stale inventory** (2 Level 3 tasks, **registered and closed on 2026-10-26**): the
      title at registration time, “clear existing duplicate same-name definitions,” was a
      **mislabel**—the `--strict` violations come only from criterion C (entry-point wiring), while
      criterion A (same-name definitions, 181 entries) is report-only and never makes `--strict`
      red; it was corrected during closure. Root cause: `STAGE_MARKERS` was calibrated on 2026-10-06
      against the pre-P4 state of “six paths wired manually”; its comment explicitly says “this
      table is deleted with the inventory after P4 unifies the Driver,” but after P4 landed, neither
      the vocabulary nor the 19 `KNOWN_WIRING_SITES` entries were retired. Consequently, the
      criterion classified the Driver's **own phase table** (`Stage::DeadCodeAnalysis` variant name,
      `slug` string table, `state.rs` dependency table) as a violation, exactly opposite criterion
      C's rule text (“adding the Nth entry point **instead of registering it in the declarative
      phase table** → violation”). Disposition = **rebias the criterion** rather than adding
      whitelist entries. Result: `--strict` violations 9→0, inventory 19→**7**, measured wiring
      points 18→**8** (7 inventory + 1 dispatch point), criterion-A same-name definitions 181→180
      (the duplicate `build_registry` entry was deleted), `pub(crate)` 167→166. **Owning phase: P9;
      Blocks the mainline: No (blocks P9 conversion to hard failure).**
  - 9.12.1 Rebiasing delivery (four commits, each detailed in the [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)
    closure transcript): ① **criterion rebiasing**—LEX merged into `Parsing` (`src/driver/stage.rs`
    and 02 §Target Design 1 both state that `Parsing` = “lexical + syntactic analysis (lex →
    parse)”), DEADCODE/MONO recognize call forms only, `#[cfg(test)] mod` is exempted by **brace
    pairing**, `dispatch` is listed separately as `DECLARED_DISPATCH_POINTS`, and 13 stale inventory
    entries are cleared (each with a removal reason, empirically divided into three categories: 5
    deleted functions / 5 functions still present but with no phase primitive / 3 entries downgraded
    to a single phase solely because LEX was merged); ② **arm forms derived**—the 12 variants'
    `self.<arm>(` forms are derived from the exhaustive `dispatch` match (`Stage::ALL` supplies the
    variants, one source) + three fail-loud guards (variant source invalid / arm not found / arm has
    no `fn` definition), and fixes a false positive caused by counting the signature line in the
    measured body (making `fn check_project(` self-match TC) (`fn_body` removes the signature
    section; measured “≥2 → <2 phases” reduction is 0); ③ **coverage declaration converted to
    fact**—the phase implementations of 4 variants (VendorConsistency / Discovery / Registry /
    RoleClassification) are shared primitives outside the Driver and are registered as known blind
    spots; ④ **blind-spot sections classified by nature**—live wiring (`collect_package_refs`,
    called at `driver/arms.rs:626`) and dead-code candidates (`build_registry`, zero callers) cannot
    receive the same treatment. Three independent reviews determined needs_revision (medium → medium
    → low, with all three findings genuinely fixed), and the fourth review **passed** with no
    findings. **Acceptance**: `python scripts/ci/check-fanout.py --strict` exit 0 (violations 0 /
    inventory 6 / dispatch points 1) ✅
  - 9.12.2 Expand criterion-C coverage and clear `build_registry` (**completed; closure on
    2026-10-26**; user adjudication **D1-A inclusion + D2-A deletion**): ① **delete** the
    zero-caller chain `build_registry` / `build_registry_from` (formerly `orchestrator.rs:379-396`)
    and the now-orphaned `discover` (formerly `:416-418`), while retaining `DiscoveredFile` /
    `Discovery` / `extract_module_info` / `parse_file`; because `build_registry` was a **lib-target
    public item** through `src/lib.rs:27 pub mod frontend` →
    `frontend/module/mod.rs:20 pub mod orchestrator`, deletion required separate adjudication as an
    external API-surface change. ② **Include** phase primitives outside the Driver that still exist
    after deletion: VendorConsistency ← `ensure_vendor_consistency`, Discovery ←
    `discover_with_used`, RoleClassification ← `roles::classify` (narrowed to a qualified form—a
    bare name would hit same-named helpers in other modules; a reproducible construction example was
    measured), Registry ← `extract_module_info` (do not use a bare `.register(`; the same name
    exists in multiple places). ③ Exactly one newly exposed site: `collect_package_refs`
    (`driver/helpers.rs:1057`, live code, called at `driver/arms.rs:626`) is registered, inventory
    **6→7**, and measured wiring points 7→8. ④ The criterion docstring's coverage declaration is
    converted to fact: the old “6+6” division becomes **10+2**, R1–R3 decidable inclusion criteria
    are added, “any function that constructs this phase's data is excluded” is deleted because five
    table entries disprove it, and three stale line numbers / unmeasured figures and two inaccurate
    comments are fixed (`orchestrator.rs:7` module header, `statements.rs:737` reference to the
    deleted function). Behavior-neutral: corpus 356 rows byte-for-byte identical, 216 snapshots zero
    drift, `cargo test --lib` 2855/0/2, `pub(crate)` 167→166 (the necessary result of deleting
    `discover`). Review chain: round 1 needs_revision (1 medium + 2 low) → fix → round 2 **pass**
    (independently reproduced with a self-made probe); then an F4/F5/F6/F8 cleanup round → narrow
    review **pass** (AST statement-by-statement comparison confirms no collateral changes to the
    criterion). **Owning phase: P9; Blocks the mainline: No.**

## Implementation Points: Dependencies and Parallelization

### Mandatory Serial Main Chain

`P0 → P1 → P2 → P3 → P3.5 → P4 → P5 → P6 → (P7 ∥ P8) → P9 → P10`

> **P3 stop-the-bleeding path (decision on 2026-10-05)**: P3's prerequisites are narrowed to
> **2.3.2 + 2.4.1 + 2.4.3** (multi-file corpus layer + two red criteria). The rest of P2 (2.1 IR
> verifier, 2.2 snapshots, 2.3.1/2.3.3 differential and performance baseline) can proceed in
> parallel with P3. Stopping the bleeding from correctness defects must not be blocked by
> criterion-infrastructure construction; however, **P4 must still wait for all of P2**—the unified
> Driver requires all three layers of criteria to be in place.

| Constraint                | Reason                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| P0 → P1                   | The three prohibitions constrain every action in P1 (1.2.x is exactly the act of adding `mod` declarations to `mod.rs`)                                                                                                                                  |
| P1 → P2                   | Reviving tests changes the test count and corpus baseline                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| P2 (after narrowing) → P3 | Vulnerability criteria must **first be written in red** (2.4.1/2.4.3) + the multi-file corpus layer (2.3.2) must exist for the red tests to have somewhere to run                                                                                        |
| P3 → P4                   | Fix bugs before refactoring; reversing the order would cement bugs into the phase table as “established behavior”                                                                                                                                        |
| P3.5 → P4                 | RFC-029g (#399)'s deletion surface covers the rewrite targets of P4 4.2.2 / P5 / P6 / P7 / P8 (the list appears in §P3.5 of this document)—reversing the order means redoing the same deletions on the new grammar, new checker layout, and new IR (D56) |
| P4 → P5                   | The `include!` refactor and checker decomposition modify the same file                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| P5 → P6                   | Consecutive changes to the same file must be separated; otherwise regressions cannot be located by bisection                                                                                                                                             |
| P6 → P7 / P8              | If type representations are not converged first, the new IR will grow into a third representation                                                                                                                                                        |
| P7/P8 → P9                | The 9.5 snapshot baseline and the 9.3 `pub(crate)` leak count reach their final form only after P7/P8                                                                                                                                                    |

### Parallelization Groups

| Group                                  | Members                                                                            | Basis                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| -------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A (the only one authorized by RFC)** | P7 ∥ P8                                                                            | Disjoint file sets: `ir.rs`/`ir_gen.rs`/`bytecode.rs`/`translator.rs` vs `lexer/*`/`parser/*`                                                                                                                                                            |
| B                                      | 1.2.1 ∥ 1.2.2 ∥ 1.2.3 ∥ 1.2.4                                                      | Each independently adds a `mod`; revert file by file                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| C                                      | 2.1 ∥ 2.2 ∥ 2.3.1 ∥ 2.3.2 ∥ 2.4                                                    | The three layers of criteria are independent                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| D                                      | 4.4 (proof-layer wrap-up) ∥ the entire P6 track                                    | `02` explicitly says S5 “should proceed independently of S1-S4”                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| E                                      | 4.2.5 ∥ 4.2.6 ∥ 4.2.8                                                              | All are independent after 4.1                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| F                                      | 9.3 ∥ 9.9 ∥ 9.10 (the three scripts introduced in P0 can be developed in parallel) | The 9.5 snapshot baseline must be taken last                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| G                                      | S3 ∥ S4 ∥ S5                                                                       | Disjoint file sets (verified on 2026-10-05: S3 modifies `docs/src/dev/design/check/` + `config.js`; S4 modifies `docs/src/rfc/` + the `check_tracking.py` generator; S5 modifies two `pub` sites in `src/frontend/core/typecheck/`; no pairwise overlap) |

**Cannot be parallelized (file overlap)**: P1(1.1.x) ∥ P8(8.0.x); 1.2.1 ∥ 8.3.2; 6.0 ∥ S2 (the same
batch of B6/E5 files); 4.3.2 ∥ 9.2 (now unified under P4); **3.4 ∥ P3.5** (`checker.rs` /
`environment.rs` are the same files—the external track's pub-deletion batch and the silent-channel
fixes must be serial).

**New internal documentation constraint**: 6.0→6.1→6.2→6.3→6.4→6.5→6.6 is strictly serial;
7a→7b→7c→7d is strictly serial, and 7b's `Phi` arm must precede 7d.

## Key Decisions and Rationale

- **P0 is an independent phase rather than part of P9**—gates are “post-hoc checks,” while rules are
  “pre-work judgment.” P9's scripts cannot prevent “patching when a refactor was required”; only
  D0–D4's review checklist can.
- **P9 is the sole authoritative list of CI scripts**—the original G3 gap had three coexisting
  lists, making closure impossible. This table merges them into 11 scripts, each owned by one phase,
  with report-only status and hard-failure conversion points explicitly traceable.
- **P3.5 incorporates RFC-029g rather than leaving it as an “independent track to do after the
  refactor”**—its deletion surface overlaps with the rewrite surfaces of P4/P5/P6/P7/P8 (RFC-039
  D56). Deferring it until after the refactor means redoing the same deletions on the new grammar,
  new checker layout, and new IR; this is the same lesson as incorporating 7e's `2315`/`2341` and
  S4's RFC-status correction.
- **P5's steps are supplied by this document rather than merged into P4**—P4 is already the largest
  single-point risk, and adding checker decomposition would exceed the rollback granularity.
- **5.3.2 uses “no consumer changes” as its hard acceptance criterion**—the decomposition must be
  independently revertible.

## Known Limitations and Risks

- **1.3.x (fixing exposed defects) has no regression criterion**—it only says “fixed defects must
  not be rolled back,” without requiring a record of what was fixed. **Execution convention**: each
  1.3.x fix's commit message must reference the name of the fixed test and the defect description as
  evidence for later bisection.
- ~~**7b.1's granularity is too coarse**~~ **Fixed on 2026-10-05**: split into the dedicated commits
  7b.1a / 7b.1b under DoD 6.
- **8.2.1's granularity is too coarse**—about 89 reference renames; missing one will **silently
  change associativity**.
- ~~**D17 / D20 have no corresponding Level 3 tasks**~~ **Fixed on 2026-10-05**: D17 lands as 7e.1,
  and D20 lands as 4.5.1/4.5.2; D32/D34 opcode reclamation is incorporated into 7e.1, while the
  deletion action remains in P10 S6.
- ~~**The `2315` / `2341` data-loss defects were originally assigned to an “independent issue”**~~
  **Fixed on 2026-10-05**: incorporated as 7e.2 / 7e.3. The exception table (throw/try) is core
  language semantics and must not be left for future implementation.
- ~~**E2 (`lib.rs:568/582` `todo!()`) has no phase ownership**~~ **Fixed on 2026-10-05**:
  incorporated into P10 S2.
- ~~**F7 (opcode gate extractor) has ambiguous ownership**~~ **Fixed on 2026-10-05**: incorporated
  into P10 S6's generation-time gate task.

## Conflict Registration: All Adjudicated

> **The original “Conflict Registration” C1–C8 and the open questions in the various documents have
> all converged into the
> [RFC-039 Decision Register](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md) (D1–D50).** This
> document retains no pending items. The one-to-one mapping is:

| Original number | Issue                                                                          | Decision                                                                                                                                                        |
| --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| C1              | `precedence_inline.rs` is revived in P1 and deleted in P8                      | **Revive** (P1's prerequisite is revival of tests with real assertions), and delete the file with Pratt in 8.6 during P8                                        |
| C2              | “Grammar-driven” commitment vs table lookup                                    | **Fully implemented**: use LALRPOP; see “Syntax: full grammar-driven parsing” in `05`                                                                           |
| C3              | C6 category definition conflicts with S1 classification                        | **S1 is classified under C6** (revival needs only regression tests, not equivalence criteria); the C6 definition is expanded to “pure deletion or pure revival” |
| C4              | `test_release_plan_spans_consumed` whitelist                                   | **No whitelist**; the difference set must be empty; if it cannot be empty, the `ReleasePlan` contract is defective → change to `PlanId` (D20/D41)               |
| C5              | Internal differences between the two `Aggregation` functions were not verified | **P4's 4.2.7 explicitly lists this as a mandatory verification item**, to be checked line by line during implementation                                         |
| C6              | The wasm playground fix requires P4's `ProgramKind`                            | **The wasm-path vulnerability fix is incorporated into 4.2.8**; P3 handles only the three native entry points                                                   |
| C7              | Ownership of `method_def_ordinals`                                             | **Assigned to 04** (P7 batch c), see D21                                                                                                                        |
| C8              | `upvalue_count` correction and the `.42` version number                        | **Fix it + raise `VERSION` from 4 to 5** (the format header already has `MAGIC` + `VERSION` fields, `codegen/bytecode.rs:14-16`), see D17                       |
